\displaystyle\langle v\mu^{1/2},h\rangle=\langle v\mu^{1/2},P_{0}h\rangle,

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}(t,x):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\big\langle(|v|^{2}-3)\mu^{1/2},h\big\rangle=\frac{1}{3}\big\langle(|v|^{2}-3)\mu^{1/2},P_{0}h\big\rangle.

5.1.
Limits from the Energy Estimate

Based on (
1.23
) in Theorem
1.1
, the system (
1.3
)
admits a global solution
f^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v})
and
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})
, so there exists a positive constant
C
which is independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\{\left\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N+1}_{x}}\right\}\leq C,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N+1}_{x}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}{f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x}N^{s}_{\gamma}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\varepsilon^{2},

(5.7)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}{f}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x,v}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\varepsilon.

(5.8)

Here, we have used the fact that for
l=l_{0}+l_{1}
,

\left\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}{f}^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{N}_{x,v}}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N\\
|\alpha|\leq N-1\end{subarray}}\left\|w_{l}(\alpha,\beta)\partial_{\beta}^{\alpha}\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{N^{s}_{\gamma}}+\sum_{|\alpha|=N}\left\|w_{l}(\alpha,0)\partial^{\alpha}\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{N^{s}_{\gamma}}.

From the energy bound (
5.5
), there exist
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v})
and
\nabla_{x}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle f^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to f\quad\qquad\;\text{~~weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}L^{2}_{v}),

(5.9)

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to\nabla_{x}\phi\qquad\text{~~weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})

(5.10)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We still employ the original notation of sequences to denote the subsequences for convenience,
although the limit may hold for some subsequences.
From the dissipation bound (
5.8
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x,v})}

(5.11)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We thereby deduce from the convergences (
5.9
) and (
5.11
) that

\displaystyle\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f=0.

(5.12)

This implies that there exist functions
\rho_{+}
,
\rho_{-}
,
u
,
\theta

\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}f(t,x,v)=\;&\rho_{+}(t,x)\frac{q_{1}+q_{2}}{2}\mu^{1/2}+\rho_{-}(t,x)\frac{q_{2}-q_{1}}{2}\mu^{1/2}\\
&+u(t,x)\cdot vq_{2}\mu^{1/2}+\theta(t,x)\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\big)q_{2}\mu^{1/2},\end{split}

(5.13)

where
q_{1}=[1,-1]
,
q_{2}=[1,1]
.

Next, we introduce the following fluid variables

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{2}\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,\;\;\;\;u^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,\;\;\;\;\theta^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{2}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,\\
&n^{\varepsilon}=\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{1}\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;j^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{1}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,\;\;\;\omega^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{1}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle.\end{split}

(5.14)

Multiplying the first equation of (
1.3
) by
\frac{1}{2}q_{2}\mu^{1/2}
,
\frac{1}{2}q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}
,
\frac{1}{2}q_{2}(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1)\mu^{1/2}
,
q_{1}\mu^{1/2}
respectively and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{v}
, we derive the following local conservation laws

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle=-\frac{1}{2}n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon},\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{3\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle=-\frac{\varepsilon}{3}j^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon},\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\cdot j^{\varepsilon}=0,\end{array}\right.

(5.15)

where

\displaystyle A(v)=v\otimes v-\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3},~\quad B(v)=v\Big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\frac{5}{2}\Big),

(5.16)

\widehat{A}(v)
is such that
\mathcal{L}\big(\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2}\big)=A(v)\mu^{1/2}
with
\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2}\in\mathcal{N}^{\bot}(\mathcal{L})
,

\widehat{B}(v)
is such that
\mathcal{L}\big(\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2}\big)

=B(v)\mu^{1/2}
with
\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2}\in\mathcal{N}^{\bot}(\mathcal{L})
and
\mathcal{L}
is the linearized Boltzmann collision operator defined in (
5.1
).
From the second equation of (
1.3
) and the definition of
n^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.14
), we also find the equation of
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle-\Delta_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}=n^{\varepsilon}.

(5.17)

Furthermore, via the definitions of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
u^{\varepsilon}
,
\theta^{\varepsilon}
and
n^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.14
) and the uniform energy bound (
5.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\Big\{\|\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}+\|u^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}+\|\theta^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}+\|n^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{H^{N}_{x}}\Big\}\leq C.

(5.18)

From the convergence (
5.9
) and the limit function
f(t,x,v)
given in (
5.13
), we thereby deduce the following convergences

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{2}\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\to\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f,q_{2}\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle=\frac{1}{2}(\rho_{+}+\rho_{-}):=\rho,~

(5.19)

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\to\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f,q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle=u,~

(5.20)

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{2}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\to\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f,q_{2}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle=\theta,~

(5.21)

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}=\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon},q_{1}\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\to\Big\langle f,q_{1}\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle=\rho_{+}-\rho_{-}:=n

(5.22)

\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+},H^{N}_{x})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. It remains to find the limits of
j^{\varepsilon}
and
w^{\varepsilon}
. Noticing that

\displaystyle q_{1}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\bot\;\mathcal{N}(L),\qquad q_{1}v_{i}\mu^{1/2}\bot\;\mathcal{N}(L),i=1,2,3,

it follows from the dissipation bound (
5.7
), the definitions of
j^{\varepsilon}
and
\omega^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.14
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\left\|j^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}^{2}+\left\|\omega^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{\Big\|\big\langle f^{\varepsilon}(\tau),q_{1}v\mu^{1/2}\big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N}_{x}}^{2}+\Big\|\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}(\tau),q_{1}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N}_{x}}^{2}\right\}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\{\Big\|\big\langle\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau),q_{1}v\mu^{1/2}\big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N}_{x}}^{2}+\Big\|\Big\langle\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau),q_{1}\big(\frac{|v|^{2}}{3}-1\big)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N}_{x}}^{2}\right\}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\right\|_{H^{N}_{x}N^{s}_{\gamma}}{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle C.

(5.23)

Consequently, there exist functions
j
,
\omega

\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})
such that

\displaystyle(j^{\varepsilon},\omega^{\varepsilon})\to(j,\omega)\;\;\;\text{~~weakly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}

(5.24)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

5.2.
Convergences to the Limiting System

In this subsection, we deduce the two-fluid incompressible NSFP system with Ohm’s law from the local conservation laws (
5.15
), (
5.17
), the convergences (
5.9
)–(
5.11
) (
5.19
)–(
5.22
) and (
5.24
) obtained in the previous subsection.

5.2.1.
Incompressibility and Boussinesq relation.

From the first equation of (
5.15
) and the energy uniform bound (
5.18
), it is easy to deduce

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~in the sense of distributions}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Combining with the convergence (
5.20
), we get

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0.

(5.25)

The second equation of (
5.15
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{{A}}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}.

The bound (
5.18
) implies

\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~in the sense of distributions}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
With the aid of the self-adjointness of
\mathcal{L}
, we derive from the dissipation bound (
5.7
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\Big\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{{A}}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\!=\;&\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{{A}}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
=\;&\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big\|\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle{{A}}(v)\mu^{1/2},\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\Big\rangle\Big\|_{H^{N-1}_{x}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\leq\;&C\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{N}_{x}N^{s}_{\gamma}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\\
\leq\;&C\varepsilon^{2}.\end{split}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{x})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Moreover, the uniform energy bounds (
5.5
) and (
5.18
) reveal that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\|\frac{\varepsilon}{2}n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}(t)\right\|_{H^{N}_{x}}\leq C\varepsilon,

which means that

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
In summary, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})\to 0\;\;\;\text{~~in the sense of distributions}

(5.26)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Therefore, combining the convergences (
5.19
), (
5.21
) and (
5.26
), we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\rho+\theta)=0.

From the integrability of functions, we can deduce the Boussinesq relation

\displaystyle\rho+\theta=0.

(5.27)

5.2.2.
Convergences of
\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}
,
n^{\varepsilon}
,
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}
.

First of all, we introduce the following Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem, a fundamental result of compactness in the study of nonlinear evolution problems, which can be referred in
[
10
,
58
]
.

Lemma 5.1

(Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem)
.

Let
B_{0}\subset B_{1}\subset B_{2}
be three Banach spaces. We assume that the embedding of
B_{1}
in
B_{2}
is continuous and the embedding of
B_{0}
in
B_{1}
is compact. Let
p,r
be such that
1\leq p,r\leq\infty
. For
T>0
, we define

\displaystyle E_{p,r}=\Big\{u\in L^{p}(0,T;B_{0}),\partial_{t}u\in L^{r}(0,T;B_{2})\Big\}.

(1)

If
p<+\infty
, the embedding of
E_{p,r}
in
L^{p}(0,T;B_{1})
is compact.

(2)

If
p=+\infty
and if
r>1
, the embedding of
E_{p,r}
in
C(0,T;B_{1})
is compact.

Now we consider the convergence of
\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}
. It follows from the first equation and the third equation of
(
5.15
)
that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\frac{2}{5\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle=-\frac{\varepsilon}{5}j^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}.

(5.28)

Thereby, by utilizing the equation (
5.28
), the energy bound (
5.5
), the dissipation bounds (
5.7
) and (
5.23
), one has

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
=\;&\Big\|-\frac{\varepsilon}{5}j^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
=\;&\Big\|-\frac{\varepsilon}{5}j^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
=\;&\Big\|-\frac{\varepsilon}{5}j^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{5\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\langle B(v)\mu^{1/2},\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}\rangle\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
\leq\;&C\varepsilon\left\|j^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{x}^{N})}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\left\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}{f}^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}N^{s}_{\gamma})}\\
\leq\;&C\end{split}

(5.29)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. On the other hand, we can derive from the energy bound (
5.18
) that

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C

(5.30)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Notice the fact that

\displaystyle H^{N}\hookrightarrow H^{N-1}_{loc}\hookrightarrow H^{N-1}_{loc},

(5.31)

where the embedding of
H^{N}
in
H^{N-1}_{loc}
is compact by the Rellich–Kondrachov Theorem (see Theorem 6.3 of
[
1
]
, for instance) and the embedding of
H^{N-1}_{loc}
in
H^{N-1}_{loc}
is naturally continuous. Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.1
, the bounds (
5.29
), (
5.30
) and the embedding (
5.31
), we deduce that there exists a function
\widetilde{\theta}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
such that

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to\widetilde{\theta}\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(0,T;H^{N-1}_{loc})}

for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Combining with the convergences (
5.19
) and (
5.21
), we deduce that
\widetilde{\theta}=\frac{3}{5}\theta-\frac{2}{5}\rho
. Moreover, it follows that
\widetilde{\theta}=\theta
from the Boussinesq relation (
5.27
) and
\theta=\frac{3}{5}\theta-\frac{2}{5}\rho+\frac{2}{5}(\rho+\theta)
. As a result, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\to\theta\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
. Noticing that
\theta^{\varepsilon}=\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}+\frac{2}{5}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})
and combining with the convergence (
5.21
), one has

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*\text{~in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Next, we consider the convergence of
\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}
, where
\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}
is the Leray projection operator defined by
\mathcal{P}=I-\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\nabla_{x}\cdot
. By taking
\mathcal{P}
on the second equation of (
5.15
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle=-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}).

(5.32)

Hence, it follows from the equation (
5.32
), the uniform dissipation bounds (
5.6
), (
5.7
), the uniform energy bound (
5.18
) and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
=\;&\Big\|-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
\lesssim\>&\|-n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}+\Big\|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle\Big\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\\
\leq\>&C\left\|n^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\left\|\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}N^{s}_{\gamma})}\\
\leq\;&C\end{split}

(5.33)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, we can derive from the definition of
u^{\varepsilon}
in (
5.14
) and the uniform energy bound (
5.5
) that for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C.

(5.34)

Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.1
, the bounds (
5.33
), (
5.34
) and the embedding (
5.31
), we deduce that there exists a function
\widetilde{u}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to\widetilde{u}\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(0,T;H^{N-1}_{loc})}

for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Moreover, from the convergence (
5.20
) and the incompressibility (
5.25
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}=\mathcal{P}u=u.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to u\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}\to 0\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Here,
\mathcal{P}^{\bot}:=I-\mathcal{P}
.

Moreover, we consider the convergence of
n^{\varepsilon}
. From the fourth equation of (
5.15
) and the uniform dissipation bound (
5.23
), one has

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}=\left\|\nabla_{x}\cdot j^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N-1}_{x})}\leq\left\|j^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C

(5.35)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, from the bound (
5.18
), we deduce that for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|n^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C.

(5.36)

Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.1
, the bounds (
5.35
), (
5.36
) and the embedding (
5.31
), we deduce that there exists a function
\widetilde{n}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
such that

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}\to\widetilde{n}\;\;\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(0,T;H^{N-1}_{loc})}

for any
T>0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Combining with the convergence (
5.22
), one has

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}\to n\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
n\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
.

Finally, we consider the convergence of
\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}
. From the fourth equation of (
5.15
), (
5.17
) and the uniform dissipation bound (
5.23
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}=\left\|\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}^{-1}\nabla_{x}\cdot j^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq\left\|j^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{N}_{x})}\leq C

(5.37)

for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1
. Furthermore, from the bound (
5.5
), we deduce that for any
T>0
and
0<\varepsilon\leq 1

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{N+1}_{x})}\leq C.

(5.38)

Then, from Aubin–Lions–Simon Theorem in Lemma
5.1
and the bounds (
5.37
),
(
5.38
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\to\nabla_{x}\phi\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{loc})}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
\nabla_{x}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{loc})
.

In summary, we have deduced the following convergences:

\displaystyle\!\!\!\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon},n^{\varepsilon}\Big)\to(\theta,u,n)\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}{\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})}

(5.39)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
(\theta,u,n)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
and

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\to\nabla_{x}\phi\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{loc})

(5.40)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
\nabla_{x}\phi\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{loc})
and

\displaystyle\left(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon},\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u\right)\to(0,0)\;\;\;\text{weakly}\!-\!*~\text{in~}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x}),\;\text{~~strongly}\text{~in}~C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})

(5.41)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, and

\displaystyle(j^{\varepsilon},\omega^{\varepsilon})\to(j,\omega)\;\;\;\text{~~weakly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})}

(5.42)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
(j,\omega)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})
.

5.2.3.
Equations of
\rho
,
u
,
\theta
,
n
,
j
and
\omega

We first calculate the term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle,

where
\Xi=A
or
B
. Via multiplying the first equation of (
1.3
) by
q_{2}
and direct calculations, we deduce that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}\right)+\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\mathcal{L}\left(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\left(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\left(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\right)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}\left(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}\right).

Following the standard formal derivations of fluid dynamic limits of the Boltzmann equation (see
[
8
]
for instance), we obtain

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle=\;

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{3}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3}-\nu\Sigma(u^{\varepsilon})-R^{\varepsilon}_{A},

(5.43)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{L}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}u^{\varepsilon}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{5}{2}\kappa\nabla_{x}\theta^{\varepsilon}-R^{\varepsilon}_{B},

(5.44)

where

\displaystyle\nu:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{20}\Big\langle\widehat{A}(v)\mu^{1/2},A(v)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,

(5.45)

\displaystyle\kappa:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{15}\Big\langle\widehat{B}(v)\mu^{1/2},B(v)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle,

(5.46)

\displaystyle\Sigma(u^{\varepsilon}):=\;

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}+({\nabla_{x}u^{\varepsilon}})^{T}-\frac{2}{3}\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{3\times 3},

\displaystyle R^{\varepsilon}_{\Xi}:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\partial_{t}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big(\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\big(\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\big(\frac{\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}}{2}\big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle-\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{T}\Big(\{I-P_{0}\}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big),\{I-P_{0}\}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle-\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{T}\Big(\{I-P_{0}\}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big),P_{0}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle-\Big\langle\widehat{\Xi}(v)\mu^{1/2},\mathcal{T}\Big(P_{0}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big),\{I-P_{0}\}\big(\frac{f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}}{2}\big)\Big)\Big\rangle

with
\Xi=A
or
B
. Here, we have used the relation
P_{0}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2})=\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}
and
P_{0}
is defined in (
5.4
).

The relation (
5.43
), the decomposition
u^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}
and the equation (
5.32
) yield that

\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon})-\nu\Delta_{x}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})+R^{\varepsilon}_{u},

(5.47)

where
R^{\varepsilon}_{u}
is given by

R^{\varepsilon}_{u}:=\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot R^{\varepsilon}_{A}-\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}^{\perp}u^{\varepsilon}).

(5.48)

The relation (
5.44
), the decomposition
u^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}
and the equation (
5.28
) yield that

\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]-\kappa\Delta_{x}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)=R^{\varepsilon}_{\theta},

(5.49)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{\varepsilon}_{\theta}:=\;&\frac{2}{5}\nabla_{x}\cdot R^{\varepsilon}_{B}-\frac{2}{5}\nabla_{x}\cdot\big[\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})\big]-\frac{2}{5}\nabla_{x}\cdot\big[\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})\big]\\
&-\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]+\frac{2}{5}\kappa\Delta_{x}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})-\frac{\varepsilon}{5}j^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

(5.50)

Now, we take the limit from the equation (
5.47
) to obtain the
u
-equation of (
1.32
). For any given
T>0
, choose a vector-valued test function
\Omega^{1}(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
with
\nabla_{x}\cdot\Omega^{1}=0
,
\Omega^{1}(0,x)=\Omega^{1}_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\Omega^{1}(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. Multiplying (
5.47
) by
\Omega^{1}(t,x)
and integrating over
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}(0,x)\cdot\Omega^{1}(0,x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\cdot\Omega^{1}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t.\end{split}

The initial conditions in Theorem
1.3
and the convergence (
5.39
) give that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\cdot\Omega^{1}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x\to\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}\Big\langle f_{0},q_{2}v\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\cdot\Omega^{1}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{0}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. That is, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\to-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}u_{0}\cdot\Omega^{1}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u\cdot\partial_{t}\Omega^{1}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t

(5.51)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
With the aid of the bounds (
5.5
)–(
5.7
), (
5.18
) and the convergences (
5.39
)–(
5.41
), we show the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon})\to\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)\quad\text{strongly in}~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-2}_{loc}),\\[5.69054pt]
\nu\Delta_{x}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to\nu\Delta_{x}u\;\;\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\text{~~in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
\mathcal{P}(n^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})\to\mathcal{P}(n\nabla_{x}\phi)\;\;\;\;\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\;\text{strongly in}~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
R^{\varepsilon}_{u}\to 0\;\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\text{~~in the sense of distributions}\end{array}\right.

(5.52)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Therefore, the convergences (
5.51
), (
5.52
) and the incompressibility (
5.25
) imply that
u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
subjects to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}u+\mathcal{P}\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)-\nu\Delta_{x}u=-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{P}(n\nabla_{x}\phi),\\[5.69054pt]
\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0,\end{array}\right.

(5.53)

with initial data
u(0,x)=\mathcal{P}u_{0}(x)
.

Next, we take the limit from the equation (
5.49
) to gain the
\theta
-equation of (
1.32
). For any given
T>0
, choose a test function
\Omega^{2}(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
with
\Omega^{2}(0,x)=\Omega^{2}_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and
\Omega^{2}(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
. Then from the initial conditions in Theorem
1.3
and the convergence (
5.39
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Omega^{2}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},q_{2}(\frac{|v|^{2}}{5}-1)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\Omega^{2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\partial_{t}\Omega^{2}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\to\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle f_{0},q_{2}(\frac{|v|^{2}}{5}-1)\mu^{1/2}\Big\rangle\Omega^{2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\theta(t,x)\partial_{t}\Omega^{2}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}\Big)\Omega^{2}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\theta(t,x)\partial_{t}\Omega^{2}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\end{split}

(5.54)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Furthermore, with the help of the bounds (
5.5
)–(
5.7
), (
5.18
) and the convergences (
5.39
)–(
5.41
), we derive the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\nabla_{x}\cdot\Big[\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big]\to\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\theta)\quad\,\,\text{strongly in}~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-2}),\\[5.69054pt]
\kappa\Delta_{x}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\to\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta\;\;\;\;\,\,\qquad\qquad\text{~~in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
R^{\varepsilon}_{\theta}\to 0\,\;\;\;\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\qquad\quad\quad\quad\text{in the sense of distributions}\end{array}\right.

(5.55)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Therefore, the convergences (
5.54
), (
5.55
) and the incompressibility (
5.25
) imply that that
\theta\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc})
obeys

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0

(5.56)

with the initial data
\theta(0,x)=\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}(x)-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}(x)
.

Next, we take the limit from the fourth equation in
(
5.15
)
to deduce the
n
-equation of (
1.32
).
For any
T>0
, choose a test function
\Omega^{3}(t,x)\in C^{1}(0,T;C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
with
\Omega^{3}(0,x)=\Omega^{3}_{0}(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
\Omega^{3}(t,x)=0
for
t\geq T^{\prime}
, where
T^{\prime}<T
.
Then from the initial conditions in Theorem
1.3
and the convergence (
5.39
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}n^{\varepsilon}\Omega^{3}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big\langle f^{\varepsilon}_{0},q_{1}\mu^{1/2}\big\rangle\Omega^{3}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}n^{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\Omega^{3}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
\to\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big\langle f_{0},q_{1}\mu^{1/2}\big\rangle\Omega^{3}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}n(t,x)\partial_{t}\Omega^{3}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\\
=\;&-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}n_{0}\Omega^{3}_{0}(x){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}n(t,x)\partial_{t}\Omega^{3}(t,x){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}t\end{split}

(5.57)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Moreover, the convergence (
5.42
) implies that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot j^{\varepsilon}\to\nabla_{x}\cdot j\;\;\;\text{~~weakly}{\text{~in}~L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{x})}

(5.58)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
Then, the convergences (
5.57
), (
5.58
) and the fourth equation in (
5.15
) imply that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{n}+\nabla_{x}\cdot j=0.

(5.59)

Furthermore, the convergences (
5.39
), (
5.40
) and the equation
(
5.53
) show that

\displaystyle-\Delta_{x}\phi=n

(5.60)

with the initial date
n_{0}(x)=-\Delta_{x}\phi_{0}(x)
.

Finally, we derive the Ohm’s law and energy equivalence relation in the second to the last line of (
1.32
).
To do so, we refer back to (
5.2
) for the linearized Boltzmann collision operator
\mathscr{L}
.
Before proceeding, let’s introduce some qualities and their properties.
For the qualities
\Phi(v)=v\mu^{1/2}
and
\Psi(v)=(\frac{|v|^{2}}{2}-\frac{3}{2})\mu^{1/2}
, there exist unique
\widetilde{\Phi}(v)
and
\widetilde{\Psi}(v)
such that
\mathscr{L}\widetilde{\Phi}(v)=\Phi(v)
with
\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\in\mathcal{N}^{\bot}(\mathscr{L})
and
\mathscr{L}\widetilde{\Psi}(v)=\Psi(v)
with
\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\in\mathcal{N}^{\bot}(\mathscr{L})
.
Furthermore, there exist two scalar functions
\alpha
,
\beta:\mathbb{R}^{+}\to\mathbb{R}
(see
[
6
]
) such that

\widetilde{\Phi}(v)=\alpha(|v|)\Phi(v)\;\;\text{and}\;\;\widetilde{\Psi}(v)=\beta(|v|)\Psi(v).

Via multiplying the first equation of (
1.3
) by
q_{1}
and direct calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathscr{L}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1})=\;&-\varepsilon\partial_{t}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1})-v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1})-2\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot v\mu^{1/2}+\varepsilon\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2})\\
&-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot v(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2})+\mathcal{T}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}).\end{split}

(5.61)

Taking the
L^{2}_{v}
inner product with (
5.61
) by
\widetilde{\Phi}(v)
,
\frac{2}{3}\widetilde{\Psi}(v)
, we obtain

\displaystyle j^{\varepsilon}=\;

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}-\sigma(\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})+R^{\varepsilon}_{j},

(5.62)

\displaystyle\omega^{\varepsilon}=\;

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)+R^{\varepsilon}_{\omega},

(5.63)

where

\displaystyle\sigma:=\;

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\Big\langle\widetilde{\Phi}(v),\Phi(v)\Big\rangle,

(5.64)

\displaystyle R^{\varepsilon}_{j}:=\;

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}^{\bot}u^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\Big\langle\partial_{t}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle-\Big\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot v(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\Big\langle\mathcal{T}\big(\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\big),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle+\Big\langle\mathcal{T}\big(\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\big),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\Big\langle\mathcal{T}\big(\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\big),\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\Big\rangle,

\displaystyle R^{\varepsilon}_{\omega}:=\;

\displaystyle n^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\varepsilon}+\theta^{\varepsilon})-\frac{2\varepsilon}{3}\Big\langle\partial_{t}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle-\frac{2}{3}\Big\langle v\cdot\nabla_{x}(\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{2\varepsilon}{3}\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle-\frac{\varepsilon}{3}\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon}\cdot v(f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2}),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{3}\Big\langle\mathcal{T}\big(\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\big),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle+\frac{2}{3}\Big\langle\mathcal{T}\big(\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\big),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{3}\Big\langle\mathcal{T}\big(\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{2},\{\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{P}\}f^{\varepsilon}\cdot q_{1}\big),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\Big\rangle.

Here, we make use of the definition of
\mathbf{P}f^{\varepsilon}
, the fact
\mathcal{T}(\mu^{1/2},\mu^{1/2})=0
,

\big\langle\Phi(v),\widetilde{\Psi}(v)\big\rangle=0
and the self-adjointness of
\mathscr{L}
.
Applying the bounds (
5.5
)–(
5.7
), (
5.18
) and the convergences (
5.39
)–(
5.41
), we obtain the following convergences

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}n^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}u^{\varepsilon}\to nu\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\;\text{strongly in}\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
\sigma(\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n^{\varepsilon}+\nabla_{x}\phi^{\varepsilon})\to\sigma(\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n+\nabla_{x}\phi)\quad\quad\;\text{strongly in}~~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-2}),\\[5.69054pt]
R^{\varepsilon}_{j}\to 0\;\;\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\qquad\quad\quad\;\,\;\,\text{in the sense of distributions},\\[5.69054pt]
n^{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta^{\varepsilon}-\frac{2}{5}\rho^{\varepsilon}\Big)\to n\theta\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\;\;\text{strongly in}~~\;C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{loc}^{N-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
R^{\varepsilon}_{\omega}\to 0\;\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\qquad\quad\quad\quad\text{in the sense of distributions}\end{array}\right.

(5.65)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. As a result, the convergences (
5.42
), (
5.65
) and the equation (
5.62
) imply that

\displaystyle j=nu-\sigma\Big(\nabla_{x}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n\Big)

(5.66)

with the initial data

\displaystyle j(0,x)=\;

\displaystyle n_{0}(x)u_{0}(x)-\sigma\Big(\nabla_{x}\phi_{0}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n_{0}(x)\Big)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle-\Delta_{x}\phi_{0}(x)\mathcal{P}u_{0}(x)-\sigma\Big(\nabla_{x}\phi_{0}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}\Delta_{x}\phi_{0}(x)\Big).

Moreover, the convergences (
5.42
), (
5.65
) and the equation (
5.63
) show that

\displaystyle\omega=n\theta

(5.67)

with
the initial data

\displaystyle\omega(0,x)=n(0,x)\theta(0,x)=-\Delta_{x}\phi_{0}(x)\Big(\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}(x)-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}(x)\Big).

5.2.4.
Summarization

Collecting all above convergence results, we have shown that

\displaystyle\left(\rho,u,\theta,n,j,\omega\right)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N-1}_{loc}),

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\phi(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N+1}_{x})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H^{N}_{loc})

satisfy the following two-fluid incompressible NSFP system with Ohm’s law

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\nu\Delta_{x}u+\nabla_{x}P=-\frac{1}{2}n\nabla_{x}\phi,&\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+u\cdot\nabla_{x}\theta-\kappa\Delta_{x}\theta=0,&\rho+\theta=0,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{t}{n}+u\cdot\nabla_{x}n-\frac{\sigma}{2}\Delta_{x}n+\sigma n=0,&-\Delta_{x}\phi=n,\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle j=nu-\sigma\Big(\nabla_{x}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x}n\Big),&\omega=n\theta,\end{array}\right.

with the initial data

\displaystyle\begin{split}u(0,x)=\mathcal{P}u_{0}(x),\;\;\theta(0,x)=\frac{3}{5}\theta_{0}(x)-\frac{2}{5}\rho_{0}(x),\;\;\nabla_{x}\phi(0,x)=\nabla_{x}\phi_{0}(x).\end{split}

Actually, by mollifying the above system and combining the local existence theory, the standard energy method and the continuity argument, we can show that
(\rho,u,\theta,n,\omega)\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{x}^{N})
,
\nabla_{x}\phi\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{x}^{N+1})
and
j\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+};H_{x}^{N-1})
, cf. Chapter 3.2 in
[
55
]
, and then (
1.31
) holds true. Moreover, by approximating
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{x}
by bounded
region
[
14
]
and the uniform bounds of functions and their limits proved above, the convergences in (
1.29
) are thus verified.
This completes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.
